\subsection{Zero knowledge: what the blinders hide and what they do not}
\label{sec:ipa-zk}

The transcript of one opening consists of group elements, challenges
and two final scalars. The group elements are hidden by the blinders:
the commitment blinder $r$ makes $P$ uniform in $\G$ whatever
$\mathbf a$ is (perfect hiding, \emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``The Pedersen
commitment'' and \S``Pedersen vector commitments''), and the round
blinders $\ell_j, \rho_j$ make every $L_j$ and $R_j$ uniform and
independent of everything else for the same reason, since each carries
its own fresh $[\ell_j]H$ or $[\rho_j]H$. The aggregated blinder $r^{*}$
is uniform too, being $r$ plus a combination of the fresh $\ell_j$ and
$\rho_j$. The final scalar is not hidden by these blinders.

\begin{remark}[Folding alone is not zero knowledge]\label{rem:ipa-not-zk}
The final scalar $a = \mathbf a^{(0)}$ is a linear form in the
committed coefficients whose coefficients the verifier knows. Follow
one coefficient $a_i$ through the $k$ folds exactly as the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:ipa-structured} followed $G_m$: the coefficient side
folds with the weights $(u_j, u_j^{-1})$, the \emph{inverse} of the
generator side's $(u_j^{-1}, u_j)$, so $a_i$ reaches the single final
entry multiplied by $\prod_\ell u_\ell^{(-1)^{\beta_{\ell-1}}}$, the
factor $u_\ell$ when the digit $\beta_{\ell-1}$ of $i$ is $0$ and
$u_\ell^{-1}$ when it is $1$. Writing $\mathbf t$ for this vector,
$t_i = s_i^{-1}$ entry by entry, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ipa-linear-form}
\mathbf a^{(0)} = \ip{\mathbf a}{\mathbf t},
\qquad
t_i := \prod_{\ell=1}^{k} u_\ell^{(-1)^{\beta_{\ell-1}}} = s_i^{-1} ;
\qquad
\text{at } d = 4:\ \mathbf t = (u_1 u_2,\; u_1^{-1}u_2,\;
  u_1 u_2^{-1},\; u_1^{-1}u_2^{-1}).
\end{equation}
% ipa_toy.py, block (3): coefficient-side linear form t = componentwise
%   inverse of s: t_sym = [70, 22, 75, 79]
% unmasked: symmetric a^(0) = <a, t_sym> = 33 = 33
% ipa_extract.py, block "the final scalar is a linear form of a":
% sym: four transcripts with (u_first, u_last) = [(63, 29), (79, 7), (34, 1), (49, 74)]:
%   final scalars [88, 22, 40, 14] = <a, t> each; solving the 4 x 4 system recovers a = [90, 61, 22, 24]
%   (u_first is the challenge of the first round, round 2: the pairs are (u_2, u_1))
In the toy of \S\ref{sec:ipa-toy}, with challenges $u_2 = 52$ and
$u_1 = 20$, the vector is $\mathbf t = (70, 22, 75, 79)$, the entrywise
inverse of the structured scalars $\mathbf s = (79, 75, 22, 70)$, and the
final scalar $33$ is
\[
\ip{(90, 61, 22, 24)}{(70, 22, 75, 79)}.
\]
One opening thus
hands the verifier one linear equation in the four secret coefficients;
every further opening of the same commitment under fresh challenges
hands him another, and $d$ of them determine $\mathbf a$ outright.
Four openings of the same vector, with challenge pairs
$(u_2, u_1) = (63, 29)$, $(79, 7)$, $(34, 1)$ and $(49, 74)$, end in the
scalars $88$, $22$, $40$ and $14$, and solving the $4 \times 4$ system
recovers $(90, 61, 22, 24)$. Blinding the commitment and the round
points hides those points, not this scalar. The masked opening below
therefore does not fold $\mathbf a$ itself: it first masks the
polynomial by an independently random polynomial vanishing at the
opening point, so that the linear form is taken of a vector the verifier
cannot relate to $\mathbf a$. The mask's commitment $S$ and the
challenge that follows it are essential to the zero-knowledge argument
below.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{The masked opening.}
The masked opening runs Construction~\ref{con:ipa-commit} on a masked
statement.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt]
\item \textbf{Mask.} Before any folding, the prover samples a
  polynomial $s(X) = \sum_{i=0}^{d-1} m_i X^{i}$ of degree below $d$
  with $s(x) = 0$: she draws the coefficient vector $\mathbf m$
  uniformly from $\F^{d}$ and then replaces $m_0$ by $m_0 - s(x)$,
  which makes $\mathbf m$ uniform on the hyperplane
  $\{\mathbf m : \ip{\mathbf m}{\mathbf b(x)} = 0\}$. She samples a
  blinder $r_s$ and sends
  \[
  S := \Comm(s;\, r_s) = \ip{\mathbf m}{\mathbf G} + [r_s]H .
  \]
\item \textbf{Challenge.} The verifier sends a uniform $\xi \in \F$.
\item \textbf{Opening.} By linearity of the commitment (\emph{Crypto
  Guide}, \S``Pedersen vector commitments'', the remark that linearity
  survives),
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:ipa-sym-masked}
  P + [\xi]S = \Comm(p + \xi s;\, r + \xi r_s),
  \end{equation}
  a commitment to the coefficient vector $\mathbf a + \xi\,\mathbf m$ of
  the polynomial $p + \xi s$, whose value at $x$ is
  $p(x) + \xi\,s(x) = v$. The parties run
  Construction~\ref{con:ipa-commit} on the statement
  $(P + [\xi]S, x, v)$ with the witness
  $(\mathbf a + \xi\,\mathbf m,\, r + \xi r_s)$, the verifier sampling
  $U$ in its setup step, after $S$ and $\xi$.
\item \textbf{Final message and check.} The prover sends the final
  scalar $c := (\mathbf a + \xi\,\mathbf m)^{(0)}$ and the aggregated
  blinder $f := r + \xi r_s + \sum_{j=1}^{k}(u_j^{2}\ell_j
  + u_j^{-2}\rho_j)$. The verifier accepts if and only if
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:ipa-masked-final}
  P + [\xi]S + [v]U + \sum_{j=1}^{k}\bigl([u_j^{2}]L_j + [u_j^{-2}]R_j\bigr)
  \;\overset{?}{=}\;
  [c]\,G^{(0)} + [f]H + [c \cdot b^{(0)}]\,U ,
  \end{equation}
  which is~\eqref{eq:ipa-final} for the masked statement.
\end{enumerate}
Completeness is that of Construction~\ref{con:ipa-commit}, since the
witness satisfies $\Rel_{\mathrm{IPA}}$ for the masked statement; the
mask leaves the opened value unchanged because $s(x) = 0$. The
transcript of one opening is
\[
(S;\; L_k, R_k, \dots, L_1, R_1;\; c, f)
\qquad\text{with the challenges}\qquad
(\xi, U, u_k, \dots, u_1).
\]

\paragraph{The hiding argument.}
The final scalar is the linear form~\eqref{eq:ipa-linear-form}, taken
of the masked vector:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ipa-sym-masked-scalar}
c = \ip{\mathbf a + \xi\,\mathbf m}{\mathbf t}
  = \ip{\mathbf a}{\mathbf t} + \xi\,\ip{\mathbf m}{\mathbf t} .
\end{equation}
% ipa_toy.py, block (4):
% mask m = [66, 23, 23, 88], r_s = 95, S = (70, 71); xi = 29; masked vector a + xi m = [64, 49, 10, 54], <., b(z)> = 59 = v
% U and round challenges (u_2, u_1) = (52, 20) and round blinders of (1)
% final scalars: c = 1 = <a + xi m, t_sym>, f = 79
% second mask m' = [32, 68, 64, 88]: c' = 75 != c = 1; accepted
In the toy, with $\mathbf m = (66, 23, 23, 88)$, for which $s(20) = 0$,
and $\xi = 29$, the masked vector is $(64, 49, 10, 54)$, its inner
product with $\mathbf b(20)$ is $59$, and under the challenges
$u_2 = 52$, $u_1 = 20$ of the unmasked run the final scalar is
$c = 1 = \ip{(64, 49, 10, 54)}{\mathbf t}$ with
$\mathbf t = (70, 22, 75, 79)$; a second mask
$\mathbf m' = (32, 68, 64, 88)$ under the same challenges ends in
$c' = 75$, and both runs are accepted (\S\ref{sec:ipa-toy}). The same
form, on a different masked vector, gives an unrelated scalar.

\begin{proposition}[Honest-verifier zero knowledge of the masked
  opening]\label{prop:ipa-hvzk}
Let $\G$ have prime order $p$, and let the verifier draw $\xi$
uniformly from $\F$, $U$ uniformly from $\G \setminus \{\mathcal O\}$
and each $u_j$ uniformly from $\F^{\times}$. There is a simulator
which, given $(P, x, v)$ but not $(\mathbf a, r)$, outputs challenges
and a transcript whose distribution lies within statistical distance
$1/|\F| + \bigl(2/(|\F| - 1)\bigr)^{k}$ (\emph{Math Guide},
\S``Statistical distance'') of the honest one. The masked opening is
therefore statistical honest-verifier zero knowledge, not perfect, in
the sense of the \emph{Crypto Guide} (\S``The simulation paradigm and
zero knowledge'').
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Call a challenge tuple \emph{good} if $\xi \ne 0$ and $\mathbf t$ is not
a multiple of $\mathbf b(x)$. The vector $\mathbf m$ is uniform on the
hyperplane $\ip{\mathbf m}{\mathbf b(x)} = 0$, a subspace of dimension
$d - 1$. The linear functional $\mathbf m \mapsto \ip{\mathbf m}
{\mathbf t}$ (\emph{Math Guide}, \S``Bilinear forms and the inner
product on $F^n$'') restricted to that subspace is nonzero unless
$\mathbf t$ is a scalar multiple of $\mathbf b(x)$. If
$\mathbf t = \lambda\,\mathbf b(x)$, the first entries force
$\lambda = t_0 = \prod_\ell u_\ell$, and the entry at $i = 2^{\ell-1}$,
whose only nonzero digit is $\beta_{\ell-1}$, reads
$t_0\,u_\ell^{-2} = t_0\,x^{2^{\ell-1}}$, that is,
$u_\ell^{-2} = x^{2^{\ell-1}}$; each of these $k$ equations has at most
two solutions $u_\ell$, so at most $2^{k}$ challenge tuples among
$(|\F| - 1)^{k}$ are excluded. A nonzero linear functional on a vector
space is onto $\F$, and its fibres are the cosets of its kernel, all of
equal size, so it carries the uniform distribution to the uniform
distribution on $\F$. For good challenges $\xi\ip{\mathbf m}{\mathbf t}$
is therefore uniform, and by~\eqref{eq:ipa-sym-masked-scalar} so is $c$,
independently of $\mathbf a$.

In an honest run with good challenges, $S$ and every $L_j$, $R_j$ are
uniform and independent (fresh blinders), $c$ is uniform and independent
of them (the mask), and $f$ is the unique scalar that
makes~\eqref{eq:ipa-masked-final} hold once everything else is fixed,
$H$ generating $\G$. The simulator produces the same distribution in the
reverse order, the way the Schnorr simulator chooses its commitment
after the challenge (\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``Sigma-protocols''): it
draws the challenges as the verifier does, draws $S$, all $L_j$ and all
$R_j$ but $R_1$ uniformly from $\G$, draws $c$ and $f$ uniformly from
$\F$, and sets $R_1$ to the one point that
satisfies~\eqref{eq:ipa-masked-final}, namely
$R_1 := [u_1^{2}]\bigl(\text{right-hand side} - \text{every other term of
the left}\bigr)$. Both distributions
are uniform on the solution set of the one equation: the honest run
parametrises that set by the $2k + 1$ points and $c$, with $f$
determined; the simulator parametrises it by $2k$ points and the two
scalars, with $R_1$ determined; and each parametrisation is a bijection
onto that set, with $p^{2k+2}$ points on each side since $\G$ and $\F$
both have $p$ elements, because the determined coordinate is unique in
each case. For good challenges the simulated transcript is therefore
distributed exactly as the real one. The challenges have the same
distribution in both, and the tuples that are not good have probability
at most $1/|\F| + 2^{k}/(|\F| - 1)^{k}$; the statistical distance is at
most that probability.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{The gap, stated.}
This argues one opening in isolation. The proof system opens many
committed polynomials at once, under shared challenges, and the mask is
one of several blinding devices it uses; whether the joint transcript
reveals nothing is a separate question, addressed under ``Zero knowledge:
hiding the witness in Halo~2'' in \S\ref{sec:halo2}, where the mask, in
the deployed form of \S\ref{sec:ipa-halo2}, reappears as the last of
those devices.

\subsection{A complete toy opening, carried by script}
\label{sec:ipa-toy}

The toy opening commits the running example's advice polynomial,
$a(X) = 90 + 61X + 22X^2 + 24X^3$ of \S\ref{sec:arith-polys}, and opens
it at the random point $z = 20$ of \S\ref{sec:arith-random} with
$v = a(20) = 59$: the opening the proof system needs, of a column
polynomial at the verifier's random point, never of the statement
polynomial at the running example's secret witness $3$. Every value
below is computed by script and can be recomputed by hand in $\F_{97}$
and on a curve with $97$ points.

\paragraph{A genuine group of order $97$.}
The vector $\mathbf a$ lives in $\F_{97}^{4}$, so the commitment group
must have order $97$: scalars of a prime-order group live in the field
of its order (\emph{Math Guide}, \S``Base fields, scalar fields, and
the Pasta cycle''), exactly as the deployed circuit's field is the
scalar field of the deployed commitment curve. The group
$(\F_{97}, +)$ has order $97$ but trivially computable discrete
logarithms, a division, so the toy uses an elliptic curve. For a curve
over a prime field $\F_q$ to have exactly $97$ points, the Hasse bound
(\emph{Math Guide}, \S``The group structure of $E(\F_p)$'') confines the
prime $q$ to $|q + 1 - 97| \le 2\sqrt q$:
% ipa_toy.py, block "group":
% Hasse window for #E = 97: q in [79, 83, 89, 97, 101, 103, 107, 109, 113] (q = 97 excluded: anomalous)
% first curve in lexicographic (q, A, B) order: y^2 = x^3 + 3 over F_79
% #E(F_79) = 97 (counted point by point), prime, so the group is cyclic of order 97; trace t = q + 1 - #E = -17, not 1
$q \in \{79, 83, 89, 97, 101, 103, 107, 109, 113\}$. The case $q = 97$
is excluded on purpose: a curve with as many points as its base field
has elements is \emph{anomalous}, and its discrete logarithm is easy
(\emph{Math Guide}, \S``The elliptic-curve discrete logarithm
problem''). The first short Weierstrass curve (\emph{Math Guide},
\S``Weierstrass equations'') in lexicographic order of $(q, A, B)$ with
exactly $97$ points is
\[
E \colon y^{2} = x^{3} + 3 \quad\text{over } \F_{79},
\qquad \#E(\F_{79}) = 97 ,
\]
counted point by point; $97$ is prime, so the group is cyclic and every
point but $\mathcal O$ generates it, and its trace $79 + 1 - 97 = -17$
is not $1$. It is the same kind of object as the \emph{Math Guide}'s
worked curve $y^2 = x^3 + 5$ over $\F_{11}$ (\S``A worked toy
example''), one size up. With $97$ points every discrete logarithm on
it is a short search; the toy exhibits the algebra of the argument,
never its hardness.

\paragraph{Generators from a hash.}
The generators are derived from public labels by hashing to the curve,
as the deployed ones are: the label and a counter are hashed to a
candidate $x$-coordinate, and the counter is incremented until
$x^3 + 3$ is a square in $\F_{79}$. This is a variant of the
try-and-increment loop of the \emph{Crypto Guide} (\S``Hashing to a
curve point''), which increments $x$ itself; its constant-time
refinement is irrelevant to a toy. Indexed from $0$ so that the
coefficient $a_i$ multiplies $G_i$, the generators are
% G1 = (12, 25)  (try-and-increment counter 2)
% G2 = (17, 27)  (try-and-increment counter 1)
% G3 = (71, 26)  (try-and-increment counter 0)
% G4 = (49, 10)  (try-and-increment counter 1)
% H = (78, 9)  (try-and-increment counter 0)
% U = (53, 26)  (try-and-increment counter 0)
\begin{gather*}
G_0 = (12, 25),\quad G_1 = (17, 27),\quad G_2 = (71, 26),\\
G_3 = (49, 10),\quad H = (78, 9),\quad U = (53, 26).
\end{gather*}
The hashed point $U$ stands in for the verifier's uniform sample of
Construction~\ref{con:ipa-commit}. No party chose any of them, so no
relation among them is known in advance; a single discrete logarithm
between two of them would give one, since $H = [h]G_0$ yields
$[h]G_0 - H = \mathcal O$. On this curve such a logarithm is a short
search; on Vesta its infeasibility is the assumption of
\S\ref{sec:ipa-binding}.

\paragraph{The instance.}
% ipa_toy.py, block "instance":
% a = [90, 61, 22, 24] (coefficients, low degree first); a on H = [3, 9, 27, 30]
% z = 20, b(z) = [1, 20, 12, 46], v = a(20) = <a, b> = 59
% blinder r = 10; P = <a, G> + [r]H = (39, 25)
The prover holds $\mathbf a = (90, 61, 22, 24)$, which takes the values
$(3, 9, 27, 30)$ on the domain of \S\ref{sec:arith-polys}, and the
blinder $r = 10$; her commitment is
\[
P = [90]G_0 + [61]G_1 + [22]G_2 + [24]G_3 + [10]H = (39, 25).
\]
The verifier holds $P$, the point $z = 20$, hence
$\mathbf b(20) = (1, 20, 20^2, 20^3) = (1, 20, 12, 46)$ in $\F_{97}$,
and the claimed value $v = 59 = \ip{\mathbf a}{\mathbf b(20)}$. With
$d = 4$ there are $k = 2$ rounds, numbered $2$ then $1$.

\paragraph{The symmetric opening, both sides.}
Tables~\ref{tab:ipa-toy-prover} and~\ref{tab:ipa-toy-verifier} refill
the rows of Tables~\ref{tab:ipa-prover} and~\ref{tab:ipa-verifier} with
numbers, listing each party's inputs and computations; the verifier's
table never uses $\mathbf a$.
% ipa_toy.py, block "(1) symmetric fold":

The verifier's equation~\eqref{eq:ipa-final} closes: the left-hand side
$P + [59]U + [52^{2}]L_2 + [28^{2}]R_2 + [20^{2}]L_1 + [34^{2}]R_1$ is
$(54, 39)$, and so is the right-hand side $[33]G^{(0)} + [40]H +
[33 \cdot 19]U$. Each fold identity holds along the way: after round
$2$, $(72, 23) = \ip{\mathbf a^{(1)}}{\mathbf G^{(1)}} + [95]H +
[\ip{\mathbf a^{(1)}}{\mathbf b^{(1)}}]U$, and after round $1$,
$(54, 39) = [33]\,(12, 25) + [40]H + [33 \cdot 19]U$. The closed forms
of Theorem~\ref{thm:ipa-structured} agree with the folds: $\ip{\mathbf s}
{\mathbf G} = (12, 25)$ is the $G^{(0)}$ the prover reached by folding,
and $g(20; u_1, u_2) = (u_1^{-1} + u_1 z)(u_2^{-1} + u_2 z^{2}) = 19$
is her $b^{(0)}$. In a group of $97$ elements collisions among the
printed points are frequent, and nothing follows from any of them. Here
$G^{(0)}$ happens to equal $G_0$ and $R_1$ happens to equal $P$; the
masked run below and the runs of \S\ref{sec:ipa-halo2} show more.

\paragraph{A false value is rejected.}
Let the prover claim $v' = 60$ instead of $59$. The prover's messages
are those of the honest run; only $C^{(2)} = P + [60]U$ changes, by one
more $U$. The left-hand side becomes $(62, 15)$ while the right-hand
side stays $(54, 39)$, and the verifier rejects. By
Theorem~\ref{thm:ipa-ks}, a prover accepted on $v' = 60$ would yield an
opening of $P$ with value $60$ and hence, beside the honest opening, a
discrete-logarithm relation among the generators; on a curve of $97$
points such relations are a short search, and the theorem gives no
security there.

\paragraph{The masked opening of the same polynomial.}
% ipa_toy.py, block "(4) symmetric masked opening of a(X): Construction on (P + [xi]S, z, v)":
% mask m = [66, 23, 23, 88], r_s = 95, S = (70, 71); xi = 29; masked vector a + xi m = [64, 49, 10, 54], <., b(z)> = 59 = v
% U and round challenges (u_2, u_1) = (52, 20) and round blinders of (1)
% round 2: L_2 = (36, 7), R_2 = (12, 25)
% round 1: L_1 = (51, 31), R_1 = (72, 23)
% final scalars: c = 1 = <a + xi m, t_sym>, f = 79; G^(0) = (12, 25), b^(0) = 19
% verifier: P + [xi]S + [v]U + sum([u_j^2]L_j + [u_j^-2]R_j) = (46, 13) = [c]G^(0) + [f]H + [c b^(0)]U = (46, 13)
% false v' = 60: LHS (56, 70) != RHS (46, 13): rejected
% second mask m' = [32, 68, 64, 88]: c' = 75 != c = 1; accepted
The same $P$ is then opened at the same $z$ with the masked opening of
\S\ref{sec:ipa-zk}. The prover samples $\mathbf m = (66, 23, 23, 88)$,
for which $s(20) = 0$, and $r_s = 95$, and sends
$S = \ip{\mathbf m}{\mathbf G} + [95]H = (70, 71)$; the verifier answers
$\xi = 29$. The masked vector $\mathbf a + 29\,\mathbf m = (64, 49, 10,
54)$ has inner product $59$ with $\mathbf b(20)$, and the rounds run on
it with the point $U$, the challenges $u_2 = 52$, $u_1 = 20$ and the
round blinders of the unmasked run: the prover sends $L_2 = (36, 7)$,
$R_2 = (12, 25)$, $L_1 = (51, 31)$ and $R_1 = (72, 23)$, and ends in
$c = 1$ and $f = 79$. The final generator $G^{(0)} = (12, 25)$ and the
final evaluation scalar $b^{(0)} = 19$ are those of the unmasked run,
since they depend on the challenges alone. The verifier's
equation~\eqref{eq:ipa-masked-final} closes at $(46, 13)$ on both sides,
and the false value $v' = 60$ moves the left-hand side to $(56, 70)$:
rejected. A second mask $\mathbf m' = (32, 68, 64, 88)$ under the same
challenges ends in $c' = 75$ and is accepted as well.

\subsection{Halo 2's specifics: parameters, generators from a hash, the
  deferred multi-scalar multiplication, and the proof bytes}
\label{sec:ipa-halo2}

Everything above holds for any prime-order group and any $d = 2^{k}$.
This subsection, and only this one, fixes the group, the size, the
generators and the normalisation of the deployed Halo~2, states its
opening, and counts the bytes an Orchard proof spends on the argument.
Nothing earlier in the section depends on it.

\paragraph{The group and the size.}
% orchard_params.py: d = n = 2^11 = 2048; k = 11 rounds
The Orchard Action circuit does its arithmetic in $\F_{p_{\Pallas}}$,
the base field of the Pallas curve, because the statement manipulates
Pallas points and their coordinates live there (\S\ref{sec:intro-claim};
\emph{Math Guide}, \S``Base fields, scalar fields, and the Pasta
cycle''). Its column polynomials therefore have coefficients in
$\F_{p_{\Pallas}}$, and a Pedersen commitment to such a vector needs a
group whose scalar field is $\F_{p_{\Pallas}}$: that group is the Vesta
curve, whose order is $p_{\Pallas}$ (protocol specification,
\href{https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\#pallasandvesta}{\S~5.4.9.6},
``Pallas and Vesta''). The specification uses Halo~2 with the Vesta curve
to prove and verify the Action statement
(\href{https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\#abstractzk}{\S~4.1.13},
``Zero-Knowledge Proving System''), so every $P$, $L_j$, $R_j$, $S$ and
$G^{(0)}$ of this section is a Vesta point and every scalar a Pallas
base-field element.
The other half of the Pasta cycle, Pallas's scalar field being Vesta's
base field, is needed only by the optional native recursion of
\S\ref{sec:pcd}; Orchard's verifier never uses it. The committed
polynomials have degree below the row count $n = 2^{11} = 2048$ of the
Action circuit (Definition~\ref{def:plonkish}; the count is given under
``From statement to circuit: arithmetisation in practice'' in
\S\ref{sec:halo2}), so here $d = n = 2^{11}$ and the argument runs
$k = 11$ rounds.

\paragraph{Generators from a hash.}
The $2048$ vector generators, the blinding generator $H$ and the
inner-product generator $U$ are all outputs of hash-to-curve on one
fixed domain-separation label, the vector generators indexed by their
position and $H$ and $U$ by two further tags (\emph{Crypto Guide},
\S``Hashing to a curve point'', Construction ``Nothing-up-my-sleeve
generators''). With hash-to-curve modelled as a random oracle into $\G$
they are independent and uniform, and a relation among them yields a
discrete logarithm on Vesta (the same section, Remark ``Why NUMS
generators are binding-safe''). No secret is generated in producing
them, unlike the trapdoor of KZG (\emph{Crypto Guide},
\S``Polynomial commitment schemes'', under ``KZG: evaluation in the
exponent''), the point the closing subsubsection returns to. Because $U$
is a fixed parameter rather than a verifier's sample drawn after each
$P$, the deployed opening re-randomises it per opening with a
challenge $\zeta$ (``The deployed opening in full'' below).

% ks_numbers.py: |F| = p_Pallas ~ 2^254.000 (255 bits)
\paragraph{Challenges from the transcript.}
The interactive verifier of Construction~\ref{con:ipa-commit} draws
$u_j$ uniformly from $\F^{\times}$, and the halo2 book's
\emph{Protocol Description} excludes zero challenges. The deployed
verifier is a hash (``Removing the verifier: the Fiat--Shamir transform''
in \S\ref{sec:halo2}), and the transcript of the
\texttt{halo2\_proofs} implementation squeezes every challenge, $\xi$,
$\zeta$ and each $u_j$, as a full field element with no rejection of
zero. A zero $u_j$ would make the fold's inversion
fail, so the honest prover succeeds unless one of the $k$ challenges
is zero, an event of probability $1/|\F|$ per challenge, about
$2^{-254}$: completeness of the deployed argument is overwhelming,
not literally perfect, in the sense fixed in \S\ref{sec:intro-claim}.

\begin{remark}[The deployed IPA normalisation]\label{rem:ipa-deployed-norm}
The deployed opening, stated in full below, is the masked opening of
\S\ref{sec:ipa-zk} with three changes. It folds with the weights
$(1, u_j^{-1})$ on the coefficient vector and $(1, u_j)$ on $\mathbf b$
and $\mathbf G$, with cross terms $L_j = \ip{\mathbf a_{\mathrm{hi}}}
{\mathbf G_{\mathrm{lo}}} + \cdots$ and $R_j = \ip{\mathbf a_{\mathrm{lo}}}
{\mathbf G_{\mathrm{hi}}} + \cdots$, the halves swapped relative
to~\eqref{eq:ipa-LR}, and the verifier's accumulation
$[u_j^{-1}]L_j + [u_j]R_j$; it scales the cross terms' $U$-coordinates
by a further challenge $\zeta$ drawn after $\xi$; and it moves the
claimed value into the commitment as $-[v]G_0$ in place of $+[v]U$. Its
final scalars are $c$ and $f$, and its check
is~\eqref{eq:ipa-dep-final}. The halo2
book, which the protocol specification
(\href{https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\#halo2}{\S~5.4.10.3},
``Halo 2'') cites for the proving system, states this opening in its chapter
\emph{Protocol Description}. Its blinding generator $W$ is this volume's
$H$, its $U$ is $U$, and its second challenge $z$ is $\zeta$, renamed
because $z = 20$ is the running example's opening point; the book's
comparison with the scheme of B\"unz, Chiesa, Mishra and Spooner
(chapter \emph{Comparison to other work}) writes $H$, $U$ and $z$ for
the same three objects. The chapter numbers the round challenges from
the first round, $u_0, \dots, u_{k-1}$, where this volume's descending
index gives $u_k, \dots, u_1$, and states the verifier's evaluation
polynomial (its $\kappa$) as
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ipa-g-dep}
\begin{aligned}
g_{\mathrm{dep}}(X)
 &= \prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\bigl(1 + u_{k-1-i}\,X^{2^{i}}\bigr)
 &&\text{in its numbering,} \\
g_{\mathrm{dep}}(X)
 &= \prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(1 + u_\ell\,X^{2^{\ell-1}}\bigr)
 &&\text{in this volume's,}
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
with structured scalars $s_i = \prod_{\ell : \beta_{\ell-1} = 1} u_\ell$,
beside the symmetric $g(X) = \prod_{\ell=1}^{k}(u_\ell^{-1} + u_\ell
X^{2^{\ell-1}})$ of~\eqref{eq:ipa-g} with
$s_i = \prod_\ell u_\ell^{(-1)^{1 - \beta_{\ell-1}}}$.

\emph{The change of variables.} The two folds are one argument. Run
both on the same vector, the same generators and the same evaluation
vector, with the deployed challenge of every round the square of the
symmetric one, $u_{\mathrm{dep}} := u_{\mathrm{sym}}^{2}$. In one
round, writing $u$ for $u_{\mathrm{sym}}$,
\begin{align*}
\mathbf a'_{\mathrm{sym}}
 &= u\,\mathbf a_{\mathrm{lo}} + u^{-1}\mathbf a_{\mathrm{hi}}
  = u\,\bigl(\mathbf a_{\mathrm{lo}} + u^{-2}\mathbf a_{\mathrm{hi}}\bigr)
  = u\,\mathbf a'_{\mathrm{dep}}, \\
\mathbf G'_{\mathrm{sym}}
 &= u^{-1}\mathbf G_{\mathrm{lo}} + u\,\mathbf G_{\mathrm{hi}}
  = u^{-1}\bigl(\mathbf G_{\mathrm{lo}} + u^{2}\mathbf G_{\mathrm{hi}}\bigr)
  = u^{-1}\mathbf G'_{\mathrm{dep}}, \\
\mathbf b'_{\mathrm{sym}}
 &= u^{-1}\mathbf b_{\mathrm{lo}} + u\,\mathbf b_{\mathrm{hi}}
  = u^{-1}\bigl(\mathbf b_{\mathrm{lo}} + u^{2}\mathbf b_{\mathrm{hi}}\bigr)
  = u^{-1}\mathbf b'_{\mathrm{dep}},
\end{align*}
so $\mathbf a'_{\mathrm{dep}} = u^{-1}\mathbf a'_{\mathrm{sym}}$,
$\mathbf G'_{\mathrm{dep}} = u\,\mathbf G'_{\mathrm{sym}}$ and
$\mathbf b'_{\mathrm{dep}} = u\,\mathbf b'_{\mathrm{sym}}$, and the
scalings cancel in every inner product:
$\ip{\mathbf a'_{\mathrm{dep}}}{\mathbf G'_{\mathrm{dep}}} =
\ip{\mathbf a'_{\mathrm{sym}}}{\mathbf G'_{\mathrm{sym}}}$ and
$\ip{\mathbf a'_{\mathrm{dep}}}{\mathbf b'_{\mathrm{dep}}} =
\ip{\mathbf a'_{\mathrm{sym}}}{\mathbf b'_{\mathrm{sym}}}$. The cross
terms agree, up to their $H$-blinders, once the naming is unwound: the
deployed $L$ carries
$\ip{\mathbf a_{\mathrm{hi}}}{\mathbf G_{\mathrm{lo}}}$, which the
symmetric presentation calls $R$, and its weight $u_{\mathrm{dep}}^{-1}
= u^{-2}$ is the symmetric weight on $R$; likewise for the other cross
term. The single-round form holds for one round on identical inputs;
over several rounds the change of variables is \emph{cumulative},
because each round's inputs already carry the previous rounds'
factors:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ipa-cumulative}
\mathbf a^{(j)}_{\mathrm{dep}}
 = \Bigl(\prod_{\ell > j} u_\ell\Bigr)^{-1}\mathbf a^{(j)}_{\mathrm{sym}},
\qquad
\mathbf G^{(j)}_{\mathrm{dep}}
 = \Bigl(\prod_{\ell > j} u_\ell\Bigr)\mathbf G^{(j)}_{\mathrm{sym}},
\qquad
\mathbf b^{(j)}_{\mathrm{dep}}
 = \Bigl(\prod_{\ell > j} u_\ell\Bigr)\mathbf b^{(j)}_{\mathrm{sym}},
\end{equation}
with $u_\ell$ the symmetric challenges of the rounds already run.
Each deployed factor of~\eqref{eq:ipa-g-dep} is a symmetric factor
scaled, $1 + u^{2}X^{m} = u\,(u^{-1} + u\,X^{m})$, so after $k$ rounds
\[
g_{\mathrm{dep}} = \Bigl(\prod_{\ell=1}^{k} u_\ell\Bigr)\,g_{\mathrm{sym}},
\qquad
\mathbf s_{\mathrm{dep}}
  = \Bigl(\prod_{\ell=1}^{k} u_\ell\Bigr)\,\mathbf s_{\mathrm{sym}},
\qquad
a^{(0)}_{\mathrm{dep}}
  = \Bigl(\prod_{\ell=1}^{k} u_\ell\Bigr)^{-1} a^{(0)}_{\mathrm{sym}},
\]
while $G^{(0)}$ and $b^{(0)}$ carry the product itself, so that
$[a^{(0)}]G^{(0)}$ and $a^{(0)}\,b^{(0)}$ agree in both normalisations,
and the two verifier equations coincide once each run attaches $\ell_j$
and $\rho_j$ to the same cross product.
% ipa_toy.py, blocks (1), (2) and (2b): symmetric r* = 40; deployed r* = 33;
% (2b) deployed L_j, R_j = symmetric R_j, L_j in both rounds: True; closes at
%   (54, 39) = (54, 39) with r* = 40 (the symmetric run's (54, 39), r* = 40)
Table~\ref{tab:ipa-sidebyside} prints both runs on the toy's data and
keeps the blinder names instead, so the deployed run attaches each
blinder to the other cross product, and its equation differs from the
symmetric one only in the $H$-term ($r^{*} = 33$ against $40$); with the
blinders exchanged, the deployed run also closes at $(54, 39)$ with
$r^{*} = 40$. What the substitution does \emph{not} do is identify the
challenge distributions: a uniform deployed challenge is not the square
of a uniform symmetric one (``Extraction in the deployed rows'' below), so
the deployed rows are analysed with their own lemma, and the deployed
opening is knowledge sound by the argument of \S\ref{sec:ipa-binding}
with Lemma~\ref{lem:ipa-single-round-dep} at every node and two
branchings, over $\xi$ and $\zeta$, in place of the resampled $U$ at
the root: Corollary~\ref{cor:ipa-deployed-ks}.
\end{remark}

% s_dep = [1, 12, 85, 50] = (prod u_sym) s_sym: True; a_dep^(0) = (prod u_sym)^-1 a_sym^(0): True

% deployed equation P + [v]U + sum([u^-1]L + [u]R) = (5, 72) = [a]G^(0) + [r*]H + [a b^(0)]U = (5, 72)
\begin{table}[htbp]
\centering
\footnotesize
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{5pt}
\begin{tabular}{l l l l}
\toprule

\midrule

  & $L_2 = (78, 70)$, $R_2 = (71, 26)$ & halves swapped \\

  & $52\,\cdot$ \\

round $1$: challenge & $u_1 = 20$ & $u_1 = 20^{2} = 12$ & \\

  & $L_1 = (43, 6)$, $R_1 = (70, 71)$ & halves swapped \\
$a^{(0)}$ & $33$ & $85$ & $(52 \cdot 20)^{-1} = 70^{-1}\cdot$ \\
$G^{(0)}$ & $(12, 25)$ & $(34, 53)$ & $70\,\cdot$ \\
$b^{(0)}$ & $19$ & $69$ & $70\,\cdot$ \\
\addlinespace
structured scalars $\mathbf s$ & $(79, 75, 22, 70)$ & $(1, 12, 85, 50)$
  & $70\,\cdot$ \\
$g(20)$ & $(u_1^{-1} + u_1 z)(u_2^{-1} + u_2 z^{2}) = 19$
  & $(1 + u_1 z)(1 + u_2 z^{2}) = 69$ & $70\,\cdot$ \\
aggregated blinder $r^{*}$ & $40$ & $33$ & \\

\paragraph{Extraction in the deployed rows.}
The deployed fold changes the rows of the extractor's linear system.

\begin{lemma}[Single-round recovery in the deployed rows]
\label{lem:ipa-single-round-dep}
Fix one round at level $j$ as in Lemma~\ref{lem:ipa-single-round}, but
let the round fold the coefficient vector with the weights
$(1, u^{-1})$ and the generator and evaluation vectors with the weights
$(1, u)$,
\[
\mathbf a' := \mathbf a_{\mathrm{lo}} + u^{-1}\mathbf a_{\mathrm{hi}},
\qquad
\mathbf G' := \mathbf G_{\mathrm{lo}} + u\,\mathbf G_{\mathrm{hi}},
\qquad
\mathbf b' := \mathbf b_{\mathrm{lo}} + u\,\mathbf b_{\mathrm{hi}},
\]
with the cross terms named with the halves swapped relative
to~\eqref{eq:ipa-LR},
\[
L := \ip{\mathbf a_{\mathrm{hi}}}{\mathbf G_{\mathrm{lo}}} + [\ell]H
   + [\ip{\mathbf a_{\mathrm{hi}}}{\mathbf b_{\mathrm{lo}}}]\,U,
\qquad
R := \ip{\mathbf a_{\mathrm{lo}}}{\mathbf G_{\mathrm{hi}}} + [\rho]H
   + [\ip{\mathbf a_{\mathrm{lo}}}{\mathbf b_{\mathrm{hi}}}]\,U,
\]
and the folded commitment $C' := C + [u^{-1}]L + [u]R$, so that the fold
identity reads $C' = \ip{\mathbf a'}{\mathbf G'} + [r + u^{-1}\ell
+ u\rho]H + [\ip{\mathbf a'}{\mathbf b'}]U$ by the same expansion as in
\S\ref{sec:ipa-fold}. Then the conclusions of
Lemma~\ref{lem:ipa-single-round} hold with rows $(\nu^{-1}, 1, \nu)$ and
the identities
$\mathbf x_\nu = \nu^{-1}\mathbf x_L + \mathbf x_C + \nu\,\mathbf x_R$
(likewise for $y$, $w$), provided only that $u, u', u''$ are pairwise
distinct and nonzero. The deployed cross terms also scale their
$U$-coordinates by a challenge $\zeta \ne 0$ drawn before the rounds
(``The deployed opening in full'' below), which the lemma covers verbatim
with $U$ replaced by $[\zeta]U$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The fold identity is the expansion of \S\ref{sec:ipa-fold} with
the weights changed: the diagonal terms of
$\ip{\mathbf a_{\mathrm{lo}} + u^{-1}\mathbf a_{\mathrm{hi}}}
{\mathbf G_{\mathrm{lo}} + u\,\mathbf G_{\mathrm{hi}}}$ carry the
weights $1 \cdot 1$ and $u^{-1}u = 1$, and the cross terms
$\ip{\mathbf a_{\mathrm{hi}}}{\mathbf G_{\mathrm{lo}}}$ and
$\ip{\mathbf a_{\mathrm{lo}}}{\mathbf G_{\mathrm{hi}}}$ carry $u^{-1}$
and $u$, which is why they are absorbed as $[u^{-1}]L + [u]R$ with $L$
holding the high-with-low product. The lift~\eqref{eq:ipa-lift} becomes
$\mathbf x = (\mathbf a', u\,\mathbf a')$, and preserves well-formedness
since $\ip{(\mathbf a', u\mathbf a')}{\mathbf b} = \ip{\mathbf a'}
{\mathbf b_{\mathrm{lo}} + u\mathbf b_{\mathrm{hi}}}$. The matrix now
has rows $(\nu^{-1}, 1, \nu)$; multiplying the row of $\nu$ by $\nu \ne
0$ gives $(1, \nu, \nu^{2})$, and the same root count, now for a
polynomial of degree at most $2$ in $\nu$ itself, shows that the matrix
is invertible exactly when the challenges are pairwise distinct.
Everything else is as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:ipa-single-round}.
\end{proof}

The two row shapes impose different hypotheses: two challenges
$u' = -u$ have equal squares, so a triple containing them makes the
symmetric matrix singular, while the deployed matrix remains invertible
whenever the three challenges are distinct and nonzero.
% ipa_extract.py, block "a challenge triple with equal squares":
% u in [3, 94, 5] (squares [9, 9, 25]):
%   symmetric rows [[9, 1, 54], [9, 1, 54], [25, 1, 66]]: det = 0 (singular)
%   deployed rows  [[65, 1, 3], [32, 1, 94], [39, 1, 5]]: det = 28 (invertible)
Over $\F_{97}$ the triple $(3, 94, 5)$, in which $94 = -3$, has squares
$(9, 9, 25)$: the symmetric rows are $(9, 1, 54)$, $(9, 1, 54)$ and
$(25, 1, 66)$, two of them equal, with determinant $0$; the deployed
rows are $(65, 1, 3)$, $(32, 1, 94)$ and $(39, 1, 5)$, with determinant
$28 \ne 0$. Remark~\ref{rem:ipa-deployed-norm} shows that the deployed
fold is the symmetric fold under the substitution $u_{\mathrm{dep}} =
u_{\mathrm{sym}}^{2}$, but that relabelling does not carry the
hypotheses across: the squaring map $\F^{\times} \to \F^{\times}$ is
two-to-one onto the squares, so a uniform deployed challenge is not the
square of a uniform symmetric one, and the condition ``pairwise
distinct squares'' is a condition on three symmetric challenges that
has no counterpart for three uniform deployed ones. Each shape is
therefore analysed with its own rows. In the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:ipa-ks} with Lemma~\ref{lem:ipa-single-round-dep} in
place of Lemma~\ref{lem:ipa-single-round}, three pairwise distinct
nonzero challenges suffice at each node, and each sibling avoids the
earlier siblings' challenges and $0$.

\begin{example}[The extractor in the deployed rows]
\label{ex:ipa-extract-dep}
% ipa_extract.py:
% a = [90, 61, 22, 24], z = 20, v = 59, r = 88, P = (51, 31), P + [v]U = (43, 6)
% == dep fold: rows (u^-1, 1, u) on the unknowns (L, C, R) ==
% node u_2 = 78: three u_1 = [31, 71, 92], det = 70; recovered a^(1) = [48, 24], blinder 86, U-coeff 36 = <a^(1), b^(1)>: True
% node u_2 = 83: three u_1 = [91, 50, 47], det = 41; recovered a^(1) = [33, 87], blinder 52, U-coeff 50 = <a^(1), b^(1)>: True
% node u_2 = 56: three u_1 = [95, 14, 96], det = 33; recovered a^(1) = [80, 6], blinder 81, U-coeff 61 = <a^(1), b^(1)>: True
% root: three u_2 = [78, 83, 56], det = 54; recovered a = [90, 61, 22, 24], r = 88, U-coeff = 59
On the data of Example~\ref{ex:ipa-extract}, the extractor with rows
$(u^{-1}, 1, u)$ and the fold of Lemma~\ref{lem:ipa-single-round-dep}
branches over its own challenges: $u_2 \in \{78, 83, 56\}$ at the root,
and under them the round-$1$ triples $\{31, 71, 92\}$, $\{91, 50, 47\}$
and $\{95, 14, 96\}$. The node systems have determinants $70$, $41$ and
$33$ and the root system $54$, and the extractor recovers the same
$\mathbf a = (90, 61, 22, 24)$, $r = 88$ and $U$-coordinate $59 = v$.
\end{example}

\paragraph{The deployed opening in full.}
The deployed opening is the masked opening of \S\ref{sec:ipa-zk} with
the deployed fold, a further challenge $\zeta$, and the claimed value
moved into the commitment. Let $\mathbf e_0 := (1, 0, \dots, 0)$, and
write $\mathbf t$ for the coefficient-side vector and $\mathbf s$ for
the structured scalars of the deployed fold throughout the rest of this
subsection.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt]
\item \textbf{Mask.} The prover samples $s(X)$ with $s(x) = 0$ and
  $r_s$, and sends $S := \Comm(s;\, r_s) = \ip{\mathbf m}{\mathbf G}
  + [r_s]H$, as in \S\ref{sec:ipa-zk}.
\item \textbf{Two challenges.} The verifier sends a uniform $\xi$, and
  then a uniform $\zeta$.
\item \textbf{The masked commitment.} Both parties replace $P$ by
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:ipa-masked}
  P' := P + [\xi]S - [v]\,G_0 ,
  \end{equation}
  which, by linearity of the commitment (\emph{Crypto Guide},
  \S``Pedersen vector commitments'', the remark that linearity
  survives), commits with blinder $r + \xi r_s$ to the coefficient
  vector
  \[
  \mathbf a' := \mathbf a + \xi\,\mathbf m - v\,\mathbf e_0
  \]
  of the polynomial $p + \xi s - v$, whose value at $x$ is
  $p(x) + \xi\,s(x) - v = 0$. The claim ``$p(x) = v$'' has become the
  claim ``$P'$ commits to a vector whose inner product with
  $\mathbf b(x)$ is $0$'': the value is folded into the commitment,
  and the opening claims the value $0$.
\item \textbf{Rounds.} The parties run the $k$ rounds on
  $(\mathbf a', \mathbf G, \mathbf b(x))$ with the fold of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:ipa-single-round-dep}, weights $(1, u_j^{-1})$ on the
  coefficients and $(1, u_j)$ on generators and evaluation vector, and
  with the $U$-coordinates of the cross terms scaled by $\zeta$:
  \begin{align*}
  L_j &:= \ip{\mathbf a'^{(j)}_{\mathrm{hi}}}{\mathbf G^{(j)}_{\mathrm{lo}}}
    + [\ell_j]H
    + [\zeta\,\ip{\mathbf a'^{(j)}_{\mathrm{hi}}}
                {\mathbf b^{(j)}_{\mathrm{lo}}}]\,U, \\
  R_j &:= \ip{\mathbf a'^{(j)}_{\mathrm{lo}}}{\mathbf G^{(j)}_{\mathrm{hi}}}
    + [\rho_j]H
    + [\zeta\,\ip{\mathbf a'^{(j)}_{\mathrm{lo}}}
                {\mathbf b^{(j)}_{\mathrm{hi}}}]\,U ;
  \end{align*}
  the verifier accumulates $[u_j^{-1}]L_j + [u_j]R_j$, and the prover's
  blinder accumulates $f := r + \xi r_s + \sum_j (u_j^{-1}\ell_j
  + u_j\rho_j)$.
\item \textbf{Final message and check.} The prover sends the final
  scalars $c := \mathbf a'^{(0)}$ and $f$. The verifier accepts if and
  only if
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:ipa-dep-final}
  P + [\xi]S - [v]G_0 + \sum_{j=1}^{k}\bigl([u_j^{-1}]L_j + [u_j]R_j\bigr)
  \;\overset{?}{=}\;
  [c]\,G^{(0)} + [c \cdot b^{(0)} \cdot \zeta]\,U + [f]H ,
  \end{equation}
  where $G^{(0)} = \ip{\mathbf s}{\mathbf G}$ and $b^{(0)} =
  \ip{\mathbf s}{\mathbf b(x)} = g_{\mathrm{dep}}(x)$ for the structured
  scalars $s_i = \prod_{\ell : \beta_{\ell-1} = 1} u_\ell$
  of~\eqref{eq:ipa-g-dep}.
\end{enumerate}
Completeness is the invariant~\eqref{eq:ipa-invariant} again, with
$\mathbf a'$ in place of $\mathbf a$, the value $0$ in place of $v$, and
every $U$-coordinate scaled by $\zeta$; the final $U$-coordinate is
$\zeta\ip{\mathbf a'^{(0)}}{\mathbf b^{(0)}} = c\,b^{(0)}\zeta$.
Subtracting $[v]G_0$ moves the claimed value into the commitment, so
that the argument opens to $0$ and the check carries no term in $v$ on
$U$; the mask leaves the opened value unchanged in either form because
$s(x) = 0$. The halo2 book gives the reason in its comparison with the
scheme of B\"unz, Chiesa, Mishra and Spooner, which adds the value on
the inner-product generator instead (chapter \emph{Comparison to other
work}): $G_0$ is a fixed base, so $[v]G_0$ is the cheaper multiplication
inside a recursive verifier. The factor $\zeta$ has a reason of its own.
The deployed $U$ is a fixed public generator rather than a verifier's
sample drawn after $P$ (``Generators from a hash'' above): a prover who
knew $U$ in advance could plant a $U$-component in $P$ or $S$, and
drawing $\zeta$ after both re-randomises the generator that carries the
inner product, so that a planted component would have to have
anticipated $\zeta$; Corollary~\ref{cor:ipa-deployed-ks} makes this
precise.

\paragraph{Hiding in the deployed opening.}
The final scalar is the linear form of~\eqref{eq:ipa-linear-form} for
this fold, taken of the masked vector:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ipa-masked-scalar}
c = \ip{\mathbf a + \xi\,\mathbf m - v\,\mathbf e_0}{\mathbf t}
  = \ip{\mathbf a - v\,\mathbf e_0}{\mathbf t} + \xi\,\ip{\mathbf m}{\mathbf t},
\qquad
t_i = \prod_{\ell : \beta_{\ell-1} = 1} u_\ell^{-1},
\end{equation}
the coefficient side folding with the inverse weights of the generator
side as always. Unmasked, the deployed fold leaks this form as the
symmetric one does (Remark~\ref{rem:ipa-not-zk}):
% ipa_extract.py, block "the final scalar is a linear form of a":
% dep: four transcripts with (u_first, u_last) = [(16, 85), (8, 16), (69, 95), (11, 41)]:
%   final scalars [70, 24, 73, 63] = <a, t> each; solving the 4 x 4 system recovers a = [90, 61, 22, 24]
%   (u_first is the challenge of the first round, round 2: the pairs are (u_2, u_1))
four deployed openings of $(90, 61, 22, 24)$ without the mask, with
challenge pairs $(u_2, u_1) = (16, 85)$, $(8, 16)$, $(69, 95)$ and
$(11, 41)$, end in the scalars $70$, $24$, $73$ and $63$, and solving
the $4 \times 4$ system recovers the vector.
% ipa_toy.py, block (3):
% s(X) coefficients m = [66, 23, 23, 88], s(20) = 0; blinder r_s = 95; S = Comm(s; r_s) = (70, 71)
% challenges: xi = 29, zeta = 12 (the code's z); round challenges u_dep = [85, 12]
% masked vector a + xi m - v e_1 = [5, 49, 10, 54]; its inner product with b(z) = 0
% final scalars: c = 20, f = 82
% second mask m' = [32, 68, 64, 88]: c' = 46 != c = 20 (same challenges); both accepted
% masked: c = <a + xi m - v e_1, t_dep> = 20, c' = 46: the same form on a different masked vector
Masked, with $\mathbf m = (66, 23, 23, 88)$, $\xi = 29$ and the
challenges $u_2 = 85$, $u_1 = 12$, the masked vector is
$(5, 49, 10, 54)$, its inner product with $\mathbf b(20)$ is $0$, and
the final scalar is $c = 20 = \ip{(5, 49, 10, 54)}{\mathbf t}$ with
$\mathbf t = (1, 89, 8, 33)$; a second mask
$\mathbf m' = (32, 68, 64, 88)$ under the same challenges ends in
$c' = 46$, and both runs are accepted. The transcript of one opening
is
\[
(S;\; L_k, R_k, \dots, L_1, R_1;\; c, f)
\qquad\text{with the challenges}\qquad
(\xi, \zeta, u_k, \dots, u_1).
\]

\begin{corollary}[Honest-verifier zero knowledge of the deployed
  opening]\label{cor:ipa-hvzk-dep}
Let $\G$ have prime order $p$, and let the verifier draw $\xi, \zeta$
uniformly from $\F$ and each $u_j$ uniformly from $\F^{\times}$. There
is a simulator which, given $(P, x, v)$ but not $(\mathbf a, r)$,
outputs challenges and a transcript of the deployed opening whose
distribution lies within statistical distance
$1/|\F| + (|\F| - 1)^{-k}$ of the honest one.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:ipa-hvzk}, with the deployed
$\mathbf t$ and with~\eqref{eq:ipa-dep-final} in place
of~\eqref{eq:ipa-masked-final}. Call a challenge tuple \emph{good} if
$\xi \ne 0$ and $\mathbf t$ is not a multiple of $\mathbf b(x)$; now
$\mathbf t = \lambda\,\mathbf b(x)$ forces $\lambda = t_0 = 1$ and then
$u_\ell^{-1} = x^{2^{\ell-1}}$ for every $\ell$, at most one challenge
tuple among $(|\F| - 1)^{k}$. For good challenges $c$ is uniform and
independent of $\mathbf a$ by~\eqref{eq:ipa-masked-scalar} and the
argument of that proof. The simulator draws the challenges, $S$, every
$L_j$ and every $R_j$ but $R_1$, and $c$ and $f$ uniformly, and sets
$R_1$ to the one point that satisfies~\eqref{eq:ipa-dep-final}, namely
$R_1 := [u_1^{-1}]\bigl(\text{right-hand side} - \text{every other term
of the left}\bigr)$; the counting of that proof shows that the two distributions
agree for good challenges, and the tuples that are not good have probability at
most $1/|\F| + (|\F| - 1)^{-k}$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{The deployed opening of the toy polynomial.}
% ipa_toy.py, block "(3) deployed opening of a(X) with the mask S, xi, zeta":
% s(X) coefficients m = [66, 23, 23, 88], s(20) = 0; blinder r_s = 95; S = Comm(s; r_s) = (70, 71)
% challenges: xi = 29, zeta = 12 (the code's z); round challenges u_dep = [85, 12] (reused from (2))
% masked vector a + xi m - v e_1 = [5, 49, 10, 54]; its inner product with b(z) = 0
% round blinders (l_j, r_j): {2: (44, 92), 1: (95, 56)}
% round 2: a'_lo = [5, 49], a'_hi = [10, 54]; b_lo = [1, 20], b_hi = [12, 46]; G_lo = [(12, 25), (17, 27)], G_hi = [(71, 26), (49, 10)]
%          cross: <a'_hi,G_lo> = (34, 53), <a'_hi,b_lo> = 23; <a'_lo,G_hi> = (54, 39), <a'_lo,b_hi> = 83
%          L_2 = <a'_hi,G_lo> + [44]H + [zeta * 23 = 82]U = (17, 52); R_2 = <a'_lo,G_hi> + [92]H + [zeta * 83 = 26]U = (47, 40)
%          u_2 = 85 (u_2^-1 = 8): a'^(1) = [85, 93], b^(1) = [51, 50], G^(1) = [(53, 53), (41, 6)]; f so far = 73; fold identity P'^(1) = (64, 5) = <a',G'> + [f]H + [zeta <a',b'>]U: True
% round 1: a'_lo = [85], a'_hi = [93]; b_lo = [51], b_hi = [50]; G_lo = [(53, 53)], G_hi = [(41, 6)]
%          cross: <a'_hi,G_lo> = (58, 8), <a'_hi,b_lo> = 87; <a'_lo,G_hi> = (24, 9), <a'_lo,b_hi> = 79
%          L_1 = <a'_hi,G_lo> + [95]H + [zeta * 87 = 74]U = (46, 13); R_1 = <a'_lo,G_hi> + [56]H + [zeta * 79 = 75]U = (24, 70)
%          u_1 = 12 (u_1^-1 = 89): a'^(0) = [20], b^(0) = [69], G^(0) = [(34, 53)]; f so far = 82; fold identity P'^(0) = (5, 72) = <a',G'> + [f]H + [zeta <a',b'>]U: True
% final scalars: c = 20, f = 82
% verifier: P + [xi]S - [v]G_1 + sum([u_j^-1]L_j + [u_j]R_j) = (5, 72)
%           [c]G^(0) + [c g_dep(20) zeta]U + [f]H = (5, 72), g_dep(20) = 69; equal: True
% false v' = 60: LHS (67, 31) != RHS (5, 72): rejected
The toy's $P$ of \S\ref{sec:ipa-toy} is opened at $z = 20$ with the
deployed opening, using the round challenges $u_2 = 85$ and $u_1 = 12$
of Table~\ref{tab:ipa-sidebyside}; Table~\ref{tab:ipa-toy-deployed}
lists both sides with every intermediate value. The verifier's
equation~\eqref{eq:ipa-dep-final} closes at
$(5, 72)$ on both sides, with $g(20) = (1 + 12 \cdot 20)(1 + 85 \cdot
20^{2}) = 69$ and $\mathbf s = (1, 12, 85, 12 \cdot 85) = (1, 12, 85,
50)$ in the deployed shape, and the false value $v' = 60$ moves the
left-hand side to $(67, 31)$ against $(5, 72)$ on the right: rejected
once more. The deployed run and the masked symmetric run of
\S\ref{sec:ipa-toy} open the same commitment to the same value with
entirely different numbers; Remark~\ref{rem:ipa-deployed-norm} relates
the two folds. The unmasked deployed run of
Table~\ref{tab:ipa-sidebyside} and this masked opening both close at
$(5, 72)$, and that run's $R_1 = (70, 71)$ equals the mask commitment
$S$: further collisions of the kind noted in \S\ref{sec:ipa-toy},
accidents of a $97$-element group, not consequences of the mask.

opened for $P + [\xi]S - [v]G_0$, and the final scalars $c$ and $f$.
The round challenges are those of Table~\ref{tab:ipa-sidebyside}.}
\label{tab:ipa-toy-deployed}
\end{table}

\begin{corollary}[Knowledge soundness of the deployed opening]
\label{cor:ipa-deployed-ks}
Let $\G$, $\mathbf G$ and $H$ be as in Theorem~\ref{thm:ipa-ks}, and
let $U$ be a fixed generator, uniform in $\G \setminus \{\mathcal O\}$
like them. The deployed opening, with the
check~\eqref{eq:ipa-dep-final}, is knowledge sound for
$\Rel_{\mathrm{IPA}}$: there is an extractor which, given rewindable
access to any prover that makes the verifier accept with
non-negligible probability, runs in expected polynomial time and
outputs either a witness $(\mathbf a, r)$ for $(P, x, v)$ or a
nontrivial discrete-logarithm relation among $\mathbf G$, $H$ and $U$.
Its knowledge error is $O(\log d/|\F|)$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The extractor branches over two values $\xi_1 \ne \xi_2$ after $S$,
under each over two nonzero values $\zeta_1 \ne \zeta_2$, and under
each of those over the rounds as in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:ipa-ks}, with three pairwise distinct nonzero
challenges per node; the tree has $4 \cdot 3^{k}$ leaves, and the
rewinding and running time are as in that proof.

\emph{Rounds.} Fix $\xi$ and $\zeta \ne 0$, and write
$U_\zeta := [\zeta]U$. With $U_\zeta$ in place of $U$ the rounds are
those of Lemma~\ref{lem:ipa-single-round-dep}, so the induction in the
proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:ipa-ks} returns a representation $(\mathbf x; y; w)$
of $P' = P + [\xi]S - [v]G_0$ over $(\mathbf G, H, U_\zeta)$ with
$w = \ip{\mathbf x}{\mathbf b(x)}$, that is, the representation
$(\mathbf x; y; \zeta w)$ over $(\mathbf G, H, U)$.

\emph{The challenge $\zeta$.} Under one $\xi$, the values $\zeta_1$ and
$\zeta_2$ give representations $(\mathbf x_1; y_1; \zeta_1 w_1)$ and
$(\mathbf x_2; y_2; \zeta_2 w_2)$ of the same $P'$ over
$(\mathbf G, H, U)$. If they differ, their difference is a nontrivial
relation among $\mathbf G$, $H$ and $U$. Otherwise
$\mathbf x_1 = \mathbf x_2 =: \mathbf x$, so $w_1 = w_2 =: w$ and
$(\zeta_1 - \zeta_2)\,w = 0$, whence $w = 0$:
$P' = \ip{\mathbf x}{\mathbf G} + [y]H$ with
$\ip{\mathbf x}{\mathbf b(x)} = 0$.

\emph{The challenge $\xi$.} The values $\xi_1$ and $\xi_2$ follow the
same $S$ and give $P + [\xi_i]S - [v]G_0 = \ip{\mathbf x_i}{\mathbf G}
+ [y_i]H$ with $\ip{\mathbf x_i}{\mathbf b(x)} = 0$ for $i = 1, 2$.
Subtracting, $S = \ip{\mathbf m}{\mathbf G} + [r_s]H$ with
$\mathbf m := (\mathbf x_1 - \mathbf x_2)/(\xi_1 - \xi_2)$,
$r_s := (y_1 - y_2)/(\xi_1 - \xi_2)$ and
$\ip{\mathbf m}{\mathbf b(x)} = 0$. Substituting back,
$P = \ip{\mathbf a}{\mathbf G} + [r]H$ with
$\mathbf a := \mathbf x_1 - \xi_1\mathbf m + v\,\mathbf e_0$ and
$r := y_1 - \xi_1 r_s$, and
$\ip{\mathbf a}{\mathbf b(x)} = 0 - 0 + v \cdot 1 = v$, the first
entry of $\mathbf b(x)$ being $1$. This is the witness.

\emph{The error.} Beyond the $O(\log d/|\F|)$ of the rounds, the two
branchings at the root and the exclusion of $\zeta = 0$ each cost
$O(1/|\F|)$. A relation among $\mathbf G$, $H$ and $U$ yields a
discrete logarithm by the every-generator embedding, as at the root of
Theorem~\ref{thm:ipa-ks}.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{The bytes.}
% proof_bytes.py, block "per-Action block": total 22 points + 49 scalars = 2272 bytes
% proof_bytes.py, block "shared block": total 33 points + 52 scalars = 2720 bytes
% IPA rows: 23 points = 736 bytes, 800 with the two scalars
% a = 1 Actions: 2720 + 2272 * 1 = 4992 bytes
% orchard_params.py: 23 points x 32 bytes = 736 bytes; with the scalars c and f: 800 bytes
An Orchard proof is a byte string. The proofs of the $a \ge 1$ Action
descriptions that one transaction carries in one pool, here called a
\emph{bundle}, are aggregated into a single proof (protocol specification,
\href{https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\#actiondesc}{\S~4.6},
``Action Descriptions''); a transaction with Actions in both the Orchard
and the Ironwood pool carries one such proof for each. Its length is
$2720 + 2272\,a$ bytes (\href{https://zips.z.cash/zip-0225}{ZIP~225},
the \texttt{sizeProofsOrchard} field;
\href{https://zips.z.cash/zip-0229}{ZIP~229}, the fields
\texttt{sizeProofsOrchard} and \texttt{sizeProofsIronwood}; the
consensus rule is the protocol
specification's,
\href{https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\#actionencodingandconsensus}%
{\S~7.5},
``Action Description Encoding and Consensus''), $4992$ bytes for a single
Action. Every Vesta point and every field element in it occupies $32$
bytes. Table~\ref{tab:proof-bytes} lists by kind, from the per-object
counts of the Action circuit whose verifying key the consensus rules fix
(protocol specification,
\href{https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\#actiondesc}{\S~4.6},
``Action Descriptions''), every one of those bytes: a per-Action block of $22$
points and $49$ scalars, repeated for each Action, and a shared block of
$33$ points and $52$ scalars. The inner-product argument's share is the
last three rows of the shared block: the blinding commitment $S$, the
$k = 11$ pairs $(L_j, R_j)$, which are $22$ points, and the two scalars
$c$ and $f$: $23$ points, $736$ bytes, or $800$ bytes with the scalars,
once per bundle, because the complete protocol collapses every opening of
every polynomial into one opening (``The multipoint opening argument'' in
\S\ref{sec:halo2}). This is the only part of the proof that grows with
$n$, and it grows as $2\log_2 n + 1$ points.
Everything else grows with the circuit's structure and not with $n$:
one commitment per advice column, two permuted-column commitments and
one running product per lookup, the permutation running products and
quotient chunks, the multipoint commitment, and one field element per
claimed evaluation.

\begin{table}[htbp]
\centering
\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{l r r p{8.6cm}}
\toprule
kind & count & bytes & what it is \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{4}{l}{\emph{Per-Action block, once per Action}} \\

  (Construction~\ref{con:arith-lookup}) \\
point  &  3 &  96 & permuted lookup table commitments $S'$, one per lookup
  (Construction~\ref{con:arith-lookup}; unrelated to the mask
  commitment $S$) \\
point  &  3 &  96 & permutation running-product commitments: the $15$
  equality-enabled columns in chunks of $7$ (\S\ref{sec:arith-perm}) \\

The three rows marked \textbf{IPA} are
this section's: $23$ points and $2$ scalars, $800$ bytes, shared by the
whole bundle. The advice, permutation, lookup and quotient rows are the
